  "content": "SLOPTRIM ACTIVE - level: full\n\n# Sloptrim\n\nYou write prose like a careful human writer. This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice. When the file guard flags a span, fix it and say nothing. The clean prose is the only output; the process is never narrated.\n\nAfter writing a prose file (.md/.txt), run: python \"/Users/masterman/.claude/plugins/cache/sloptrim/sloptrim/0.9.0/scripts/detect.py\" \"<file>\" and read _metrics.ai_tell_score. If the band is worse than the target - clean or light tells (score <= 40) - fix only the flagged spans, at most two passes, keeping rhythm variation (a flattened husk is as obvious as slop).     {
      "name": "Workflow",
      "description": "Execute a workflow script that orchestrates multiple subagents deterministically. Workflows run in the background — this tool returns immediately with a task ID, and a <task-notification> arrives when the workflow completes. Use /workflows to watch live progress.\n\nONLY call this tool when the user has explicitly opted into multi-agent orchestration. Workflows can spawn dozens of agents and consume a large amount of tokens; the user must request that scale, not have it inferred. Explicit opt-in means one of:\n- The user included the keyword \"ultracode\" in their prompt (you'll see a system-reminder confirming it).\n- Ultracode is on for the session (a system-reminder confirms it) — see **Ultracode** in the workflow authoring reference.\n- The user directly asked you to run a workflow or use multi-agent orchestration in their own words (\"use a workflow\", \"run a workflow\", \"fan out agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\"). The ask must be in the user's words — a task that would merely benefit from a workflow does not count.\n- The user invoked a skill or slash command whose instructions tell you to call Workflow.\n- The user asked you to run a specific named or saved workflow.\n\nFor any other task — even one that would clearly benefit from parallelism — do NOT call this tool. Use the Agent tool (if available) for individual subagents, or briefly describe what a multi-agent workflow could do and how much it would roughly cost, and ask the user whether to run it. Mention they can ask for one with \"use a workflow\" in a future message to skip the ask.\n\nEvery script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`: a PURE LITERAL (no variables, calls or interpolation) giving the workflow's `name`, a one-line `description` (shown in the permission dialog) and optionally `phases` — one `{ title, detail? }` per phase() call, titles matched exactly. Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first, and do not also set the tool's `name` input (that selects a saved workflow); it is plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nBefore writing a script, load the `workflow-authoring` skill — the workflow authoring reference: script API and gotchas, resume, the **Ultracode** section, quality patterns, worked examples.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config."
    },
    {
      "name": "Write",
      "description": "Writes a file to the local filesystem, overwriting if one exists.\n\nWhen to use: creating a new file, or fully replacing one you've already Read. Overwriting an existing file you haven't Read will fail. For partial changes, use Edit instead."
    }
  ]
}

CRITICAL!!: 
- NEVER EVER EVER create a branch!!! branches are FORBIDDEN!!!
- NEVER EVER EVER delete the `bugdrop-screenshots` branch, locally or remotely. It is protected project history.
- NEVER create a worktree, UNLESS the user has specifically asked for it!
- NEVER delete files unless the user has specifically asked for it. If you think a file is misplaced, unrelated, or stale, that is an entirely separate request — ask first. "Looks unrelated to this task" is not authorization to delete.

- When the user gives a UUID and asks what is going on with it, treat it as an OmniHarness conversation/session lookup: check `sqlite.db`, starting with the `runs` row for that UUID, then inspect related `workers`, `messages`, `execution_events`, queued messages, and validation/plan records as needed.
- To delete all conversations and associated persisted artifacts, use `scripts/delete-conversations.sh`

Planning artifacts:
- When the user says “write the plan”, “write a full plan”, or “write the implementation plan”, treat that as a request to persist the complete plan to a file, not merely display it in chat.
- If the user gives a path, write the plan there. Otherwise use `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md`, using the current date and a non-colliding filename.
- The saved file is the canonical full plan. The final response should link to it and summarize the result rather than replacing the file with a chat-only plan.
- This convention applies even when the current message omits the destination because “write the plan” is the project’s established shorthand for creating the plan artifact.
- If the same request explicitly says not to change the workspace or not to create a file, obey that more specific prohibition and ask for a destination when needed.

Testing:
- After any UI/frontend change, always run `pnpm build:interface:web` before considering the work complete.
- When testing the app, use the already-running process if one exists instead of starting another server.
- The normal runner URL is `http://localhost:3050`; the Vite development interface is usually at `http://localhost:5173`.
- Clean up any test sessions/conversations and their associated persisted artifacts before finishing.
- For lifecycle / chaos-style regressions (reconnect, restart, FK-on-delete, plan-review leftover state), use `pnpm test:lifecycle`. Scenarios live under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` and drive the control plane via HTTP/SSE — no Chromium. To debug a specific reported bug, mirror it as a new scenario file.
- Read `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` before adding new server-side state transitions. It is the spec all new code is held to.
- Worker conversation content (direct-control and supervisor-driven worker turns) lives in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker at `app-data/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl`. Persist new worker content through `appendWorkerEntry` in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts`; render it through `WorkerEntriesManager` and the `entries` prop on `Terminal`. Do NOT add a parallel persistence layer (a sibling JSONL, a new `messages.kind`, an in-memory cache that the frontend reconciles against the stream). See `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.

Lifecycle observability rules (full doc: `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`):
- Every server-side decision (spawn, reattach, recreate, give up, refuse, delete, fail) emits a typed named event via `emitNamedEvent` from `@/server/events/named-events`. Silent early returns and bare `catch {}` are bugs.
- User-relevant failures additionally emit `error.surfaced` with a stable `code` (typed union in `named-events.ts`), `surface`, and at least one relevant subject id such as `runId`/`workerId`/`conversationId`/`accountId`. Never funnel through a blanket wrapper in `api-errors.ts`.
- All SSE frames carry an `id:`. Clients reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from the ring buffer or emits `stream.resync_required`. Snapshot bootstrap is `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` (anchor id in the `x-omni-last-event-id` response header).
- Dev-only event log: `GET /api/events/log?since=<id>&runId=<id>` returns the ring buffer as JSON. Use this when triaging "X didn't happen" bug reports — if the event isn't there, the server didn't do the thing, and the next step is finding the silent branch.
- Chaos is a client-side concern. Never add fault-injection code paths to server code.

Frontend i18n:
- EVERY user-facing frontend string MUST live in `shared/locales/*.json` and be rendered with the `t()` function from `@/lib/i18n`.
- NEVER hardcode user-facing JSX text, button labels, dialog titles, aria-labels, titles, placeholders, empty states, status labels, help text, error fallback text, or visible option labels directly in components.
- For every new user-facing string, add the key to `shared/locales/en.json` and every other locale file in `shared/locales/` in the same change. Use stable dotted keys like `settings.runtime.recovery` or `conversation.sidebar.search`.
- Components that render translated strings and need to update when the language changes MUST subscribe to the i18n manager by calling `useI18nSnapshot()` from `@/lib/i18n`; then render text with the existing `t("key", params?)` function. The hook is only for re-rendering; `t()` is the translation API.
- Non-React modules that produce user-visible text must still use `t("key", params?)` at the boundary where text is produced. Protocol values, storage keys, API paths, CSS classes, model ids, and database values are not translation keys unless they are displayed as copy.
- Do not pass translated strings through transactions or persist translated UI copy. Store stable ids/values and translate at render time.

React Best Practices:
- MutableRefObject is deprecated in React; use RefObject instead.
- NEVER use require() to import modules. Use import instead.
- ALWAYS centralize all state in global single source of truth Manager classes. Components/listeners subscribe to updates from Managers and use Manager methods to update data.
- Always implement a custom data manager for any data structure. NEVER use separate state variables and arrays as the source of truth.
- NEVER send data around in transactions.
- Avoid using useEffect() to update state. Prefer useCallback() or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.
- Avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.
- NEVER store string literals or UI settings for the frontend in the .env file. Use literals in files, inline arrays, or .json files instead.
- When debugging React: if a fix did not work, think hard about whether this is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:32:14.542Z

So effort is set at session launch, but for an existing conversation, changing it in the composer updates the preferred worker effort and triggers a comparison against the worker's launch effort — if they differ, the worker gets recreated from the transcript, replaying the conversation. So yes, effort changes do take effect mid-conversation, just via a recreation mechanism.

Now I want to check what "replay" actually means in `recreateWorkerFromTranscript` — whether it resumes the provider session by ID or replays a summarized transcript into a fresh session, since that determines whether the full conversation needs reprocessing.

111	    bridgeSessionId: freshWorker.sessionId ?? null,

113	    currentText: freshWorker.currentText,

118	      launchCredentialSource: launchSelection.credentialSource,

121	  }).where(workerPredicate).returning({ id: workers.id }).get();
122	  if (!persistedWorker) {

130	  if (args.selection?.accountId) {

134	      .where(eq(workerCredentialAllocations.workerId, args.worker.id))
135	      .get();
136	    if (existingAllocation) {

142	        explicit: true,

163	  await recordExecutionEvent({

41	function formatTranscriptEntry(entry: WorkerEntry) {
42	  const text = entry.text?.trim();
43	  if (!text) {
44	    return null;
45	  }
46	
47	  if (entry.type === "user_input" || entry.type === "supervisor_input") {
48	    return `User: ${text}`;
49	  }
50	  if (entry.type === "message") {
51	    return `Assistant: ${text}`;
52	  }
53	  if (entry.type === "thought") {
54	    return `Assistant thought: ${text}`;
55	  }
56	  if (entry.type === "tool_call" || entry.type === "tool_call_update") {
57	    const status = entry.status ? ` ${entry.status}` : "";
58	    return `Tool ${entry.toolKind ?? entry.type}${status}: ${text}`;
59	  }
60	  return `${entry.type}: ${text}`;
61	}
62	
63	function renderTranscriptReplayPrompt(entries: WorkerEntry[], nextUserPrompt: string) {
64	  const transcript = entries
65	    .map((entry) => formatTranscriptEntry(entry))
66	    .filter((line): line is string => Boolean(line))
67	    .join("\n\n");
68	  const trimmedTranscript = transcript.length > MAX_TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_CHARS
69	    ? transcript.slice(-MAX_TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_CHARS)
70	    : transcript;
71	
72	  return [
73	    TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_INSTRUCTION,
74	    "",
75	    "Saved transcript:",
76	    trimmedTranscript || "(No transcript text was captured.)",
77	    "",
78	    "Next user prompt:",
79	    nextUserPrompt,
80	  ].join("\n");
81	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:32:50.264Z

I'm confirming that effort does get adjusted, but not mid-session — the composer's selection is stored as the preferred launch effort, and when a message is sent, the reconciliation logic compares it against the worker's current effective effort. If they differ, it kills the existing Claude Code process and spawns a fresh one with the new setting.

As for reprocessing the full conversation — yes, but it's not a true resume. Instead of resuming the provider session ID, the new worker gets a transcript replay prompt built from the OmniHarness JSONL, flattening the conversation into User/Assistant/Tool lines, truncated to the last 24,000 characters, plus a replay instruction preamble and the new message. This means the new session treats the entire history as one giant first message — causing a full prompt-cache miss, dropping anything beyond 24k characters (oldest first), and reducing tool outputs to summarized text rather than real structured results.

One caveat worth noting: if the worker was launched without an explicit effort (so its effective launch effort is null), the effort-changed check evaluates to false, meaning nothing gets recreated and the new effort silently doesn't take hold.

There are additional edge cases too: if the worker is mid-turn and not in a switchable status, the effort switch gets deferred and the current message just runs at the old effort; and for gateway-overlay Claude, the effort-application logic explicitly skips it. Also worth flagging that this transcript replay means full re-ingestion of input tokens with no cache reuse — a real cost implication. I want to keep the response tight, cite file:line references, and format it as concise terminal markdown since this is a chat context rather than a formal document.

## Claude · 2026-09-12T11:32:58.220Z

Short answer: yes it changes, and yes it costs you a full re-send of the conversation — the session is thrown away and rebuilt, not re-configured.

## What happens on send

1. The composer selection writes `runs.preferred_worker_effort` (`src/runtime/http/routes/conversation-messages.ts:69`).
2. On send, `reconcileDirectWorkerSelection` compares it against the worker's `effective_launch_effort` (`src/server/conversations/send-message.ts:191-209`).
3. Different → `recreateWorkerFromTranscript` (`src/server/workers/provider-session-recovery.ts:23`): `cancelAgent` kills the running Claude Code process, then `spawnAgent` starts a fresh one with `effort: "high"`.
4. Effort is applied only at session start, via ACP `setSessionConfigOption` on the new session (`src/server/agent-runtime/manager.ts:1863-1889`). There's no path that changes effort on a live session.

## The reprocessing

It does not resume the provider session id. It builds a replay prompt from OmniHarness's own worker JSONL (`buildTranscriptReplayPrompt`, `src/server/workers/session-recovery.ts:83`): the whole history flattened into `User:` / `Assistant:` / `Assistant thought:` / `Tool <kind> <status>:` lines, preceded by `TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_INSTRUCTION`, with your new message appended under `Next user prompt:`.

Consequences worth knowing:

- The new session sees the entire history as one giant first user message. Full prompt-cache miss, and the whole transcript is billed as fresh input tokens.
- It's tail-truncated to the last 24,000 chars (`MAX_TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_CHARS`, line 12). Anything older is silently dropped.
- Tool calls come back as summarized text lines, not real tool results — so file contents the old session had in context are gone unless they re-read them.

## Three cases where it silently doesn't happen

- **Worker launched without an explicit effort.** `effortChanged = Boolean(currentEffort && requestedEffort && currentEffort !== requestedEffort)` (line 209). If `effective_launch_effort` is null, this is false and nothing is recreated — your "high" just doesn't apply.
- **Worker mid-turn.** Switchable statuses are only cancelled/done/error/failed/idle/stopped (line 180-182). Otherwise you get a `worker.selection_deferred` event and the message runs at the old effort.
- **Claude on a gateway overlay.** `shouldApplyRequestedEffort` excludes it (`manager.ts:1867`), so the spawn won't set effort even though the recreation still happened.

So the cheap mental model: changing effort mid-conversation is closer to "start a new session and paste the last 24k chars of transcript into it" than to "turn a dial."

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:37:24.413Z

The copy button is hover-only, which on mobile only reveals after tapping, sitting bottom-right and overlapping text. For mobile I should move it to the left side, and add a small label next to the icon showing the model and effort that generated the message.

For positioning I need to figure out which breakpoint convention the codebase uses for mobile — ConversationComposer uses a 639px max-width matching `sm`, while ConversationMain and WorkerOutputCopyAction use `md:`, so I need to check what Terminal.tsx and UserInputMessage use to stay consistent. For the model/effort label, I need per-message attribution data, which means checking whether worker entries carry model metadata, since a worker could be recreated mid-conversation with a different model or effort — I should look into entries-types and the output-store's appendWorkerEntry to see if that info is tracked per entry.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01Vgx4113BmmLey4XNvRcc1J · 2026-09-12T11:37:26.392Z

```text
/**
 * Shared on-disk and on-wire schema for the unified worker conversation
 * stream. See docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md and the
 * implementation plan at
 * docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-16-unified-worker-conversation-stream.md.
 *
 * The bridge keeps emitting its own narrower `BridgeOutputEntry` shape
 * (defined in src/server/bridge-client/index.ts). Bridge entries are
 * converted to `WorkerEntry` at the writer boundary in
 * `src/server/workers/output-store.ts#appendWorkerEntry`. Code outside the
 * writer never constructs a `WorkerEntry` for a bridge entry.
 */

export type WorkerEntryAuthorRole = "user" | "assistant" | "supervisor" | "system";
export type WorkerEntryChannel = "stdout" | "stderr" | "stdin" | "system" | "agent";

export type WorkerEntryAttachment = {
  id: string;
  filename: string;
  mimeType: string;
  sizeBytes: number;
  storagePath?: string;
};

export type BridgeWorkerEntryType =
  | "message"
  | "thought"
  | "tool_call"
  | "tool_call_update"
  | "permission"
  | "elicitation"
  | "user_message_chunk"
  | "plan"
  | "plan_update"
  | "plan_removed"
  | "available_commands"
  | "current_mode"
  | "config_option"
  | "session_info"
  | "usage"
  | "agent_content"
  | "user_content";

export type ServerWorkerEntryType =
  | "user_input"
  | "supervisor_input"
  | "system_note"
  | "lifecycle";

export type WorkerEntryType = BridgeWorkerEntryType | ServerWorkerEntryType;

export type WorkerPlanProjection =
  | "accepted_core"
  | "session_reset"
  | "rejected"
  | "unsupported"
  | "stale";

export interface WorkerEntry {
  /** Stable id from the bridge for bridge entries; uuid for server-produced entries. */
  id: string;
  /** Monotonic per (runId, workerId), assigned at write time by the writer. */
  seq: number;
  type: WorkerEntryType;
  text: string;
  /** For server-produced entries: the delivery timestamp. For bridge entries: bridge clock. */
  timestamp: string;
  toolCallId?: string | null;
  toolKind?: string | null;
  status?: string | null;
  raw?: unknown;
  authorRole?: WorkerEntryAuthorRole;
  channel?: WorkerEntryChannel;
  attachments?: WorkerEntryAttachment[];
  /** ACP session identity for plan-bearing and plan-boundary entries. */
  acpSessionId?: string | null;
  /** Explicit projection classification; payload shape never implies acceptance. */
  planProjection?: WorkerPlanProjection;
  /** Protocol/debug rows and session boundaries never enter ordinary conversation UI. */
  diagnosticOnly?: boolean;
  /** Normalized complete-list core plan, present only for accepted_core entries. */
  normalizedPlan?: import("./acp-plan").AcpPlanItem[];
}

/**
 * Collapse append-only revisions and cross-worker copies of one logical entry
 * into the item the conversation UI renders. Position comes from the first
 * appearance; content comes from the latest revision; timestamp placement
 * follows the newest worker that owns a copy of the entry.
 */
export function coalesceWorkerEntriesById(
  entries: ReadonlyArray<WorkerEntry>,
  workerOrder: ReadonlyArray<string> = [],
): WorkerEntry[] {
  const workerRank = new Map(workerOrder.map((workerId, index) => [workerId, index]));
  const rankOf = (entry: WorkerEntry) => {
    const workerId = (entry as WorkerEntry & { workerId?: unknown }).workerId;
    return typeof workerId === "string" ? workerRank.get(workerId) ?? -1 : -1;
  };

  const positionByKey = new Map<string, number>();
  const placementByKey = new Map<string, { timestamp: string; rank: number }>();
  const result: WorkerEntry[] = [];
  for (const entry of entries) {
    const key = typeof entry.id === "string" && entry.id ? entry.id : null;
    if (!key) {
      result.push(entry);
      continue;
    }
    const existingPosition = positionByKey.get(key);
    if (existingPosition === undefined) {
      positionByKey.set(key, result.length);
      if (entry.timestamp) {
        placementByKey.set(key, { timestamp: entry.timestamp, rank: rankOf(entry) });
      }
      result.push(entry);
      continue;
    }

    const held = placementByKey.get(key);
    const candidate = entry.timestamp;
    const candidateRank = rankOf(entry);
    let preservedTimestamp = candidate;
    if (held && candidate) {
      const keepCandidate = candidateRank > held.rank
        || (candidateRank === held.rank && candidate < held.timestamp);
      preservedTimestamp = keepCandidate ? candidate : held.timestamp;
      placementByKey.set(key, {
        timestamp: preservedTimestamp,
        rank: Math.max(candidateRank, held.rank),
      });
    } else if (held) {
      preservedTimestamp = held.timestamp;
    } else if (candidate) {
      placementByKey.set(key, { timestamp: candidate, rank: candidateRank });
    }
    result[existingPosition] = { ...entry, timestamp: preservedTimestamp };
  }
  return result;
}

const BRIDGE_TYPES: ReadonlySet<WorkerEntryType> = new Set<WorkerEntryType>([
  "message",
  "thought",
  "tool_call",
  "tool_call_update",
  "permission",
  "elicitation",
  "user_message_chunk",
  "plan",
  "plan_update",
  "plan_removed",
  "available_commands",
  "current_mode",
  "config_option",
  "session_info",
  "usage",
  "agent_content",
  "user_content",
]);

const SERVER_TYPES: ReadonlySet<WorkerEntryType> = new Set<WorkerEntryType>([
  "user_input",
  "supervisor_input",
  "system_note",
  "lifecycle",
]);

export function isBridgeOutputEntry(entry: WorkerEntry): boolean {
  return BRIDGE_TYPES.has(entry.type);
}

export function isServerProducedEntry(entry: WorkerEntry): boolean {
  return SERVER_TYPES.has(entry.type);
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Inline image content.
//
// An `agent_content`/`user_content` entry can carry a whole image as base64 in
// `raw.content.data`. Those bytes are conversation content, not disposable
// runtime diagnostics, and the worker stream is their only durable home — so
// the generic raw-string truncators must leave them intact, exactly as
// `toLiveEntry` already exempts assistant message text. Truncating them
// instead produced a stream whose images could never be decoded again.
//
// The payload is still bounded, just far above a realistic screenshot. The cap
// is expressed in base64 characters and sized to match the 25 MB decoded ceiling
// enforced on the read side by `src/server/workers/entry-content.ts`.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** 25 MB of decoded bytes, expressed as base64 characters (4 chars per 3 bytes). */
export const IMAGE_CONTENT_DATA_CHARS = Math.ceil((25 * 1024 * 1024) / 3) * 4;

const IMAGE_CONTENT_TYPES: ReadonlySet<WorkerEntryType> = new Set<WorkerEntryType>([
  "agent_content",
  "user_content",
]);

/** Pointer the client resolves through `GET /api/workers/:id/entries?contentEntryId=`. */

parent: 1f481526-4add-44d4-bf18-d7cdf169f448

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:37:34.955Z

Since there's no per-entry model field, I need to figure out where model and effort info per message could come from — possibly `config_option` or `session_info` entries in the stream, which come from the bridge and might carry model/effort changes or session IDs, or alternatively the worker row's `effectiveLaunchModel`/`effectiveLaunchEffort` fields.

Worker recreation reuses the same worker id and just updates `effectiveLaunchModel`/`effectiveLaunchEffort` in place, so the worker row only reflects the current model, not history. But `config_option` entries appended at session start (and possibly on changes) could give an accurate timeline of which model/effort applied to which messages — I want to check how those are produced, whether they're diagnostic-only, and also look at whether `lifecycle` entries note recreation, so I'll grep the server/bridge code for "config_option".

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:37:58.292Z

I'm tracing config_option entries appended at session start and on setSessionConfigOption calls, since these carry the model and effort exactly as reported by the CLI, giving a timeline-ordered source of truth. Now I need to check whether agent-output.ts actually surfaces config_option entries when building activities for the client.

This is turning into a multi-part task, so let me lay out a plan: first, fix the mobile copy button positioning in Terminal.tsx so it sits on the left side on mobile while staying bottom-right on desktop, using responsive left/right classes. Then I need to figure out whether the model/effort label should accompany that icon on both mobile and desktop, per the user's request.

I'm thinking the label should share the same hover-triggered visibility as the copy icon, since the current wrapper is an absolute overlay to avoid reserving extra vertical space — keeping the label tied to hover preserves that existing layout behavior rather than forcing it always-visible.

Now I need to check whether Terminal.tsx is actually the component rendering agent messages on mobile, since ConversationMain likely uses this path for direct conversations, and hover would need to map to tap on touch devices.

For the model/effort label itself, I need to trace back through preceding `config_option` entries in the worker stream to figure out which model and effort level applied to a given assistant message, and figure out how the ACP session config option exposes the model's display name versus its raw value.

160	function stripLineNumbersIfNeeded(value: string): string {
161	  const lines = value.split("\n");

 * is still writing prose, and protocol metadata can arrive at any moment. None
 * of those are a turn boundary. Treating them as one restarts the in-progress
 * message on every interleave, so a paragraph written next to a running
 * command reaches the reader as one bubble per delta.
 *
 * Everything else — a new `tool_call`, a permission prompt, user input, a plan
 * — is a real boundary and still ends the run.
 */
const MESSAGE_RUN_PASSTHROUGH_TYPES = new Set([
  "tool_call_update",
  "agent_content",
  "usage",
  "available_commands",
  "current_mode",
  "config_option",
  "session_info",
]);

export function appendOutputEntry(record: AgentRecord, input: OutputEntryInput) {
  const archiveEntry = record.outputArchive.append(input);
  if (!MESSAGE_RUN_PASSTHROUGH_TYPES.has(input.type)) {
    record.activeOutputEntryId = null;
  }
  record.outputEntries.push(toLiveEntry(archiveEntry));
  pruneLiveEntries(record);
}

 * ends the run.

/**
 * GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries?afterSeq=N
 *
 * Returns the worker's transcript entries strictly newer than `afterSeq`
 * along with the latest persisted seq. Clients track the latest

const DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT = 100;
const MAX_TAIL_LIMIT = 1000;

function parsePositiveInt(value: string | null): number | null {
  if (value == null) return null;
  const parsed = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
  if (!Number.isFinite(parsed) || parsed <= 0) return null;
  return parsed;
}

function parseAfterSeq(value: string | null): number {
  if (value == null) {
    return 0;
  }
  const parsed = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
  if (!Number.isFinite(parsed) || parsed < 0) {
    return 0;
  }
  return parsed;
}

/**
 * Trade inline image payloads for content pointers on the way out.

 * The stream persists whole images so they stay decodable forever, but sending
 * them inline would push megabytes of base64 through every transcript page.
 * The client resolves each pointer once, on demand, against the
 * `?contentEntryId=` route above.
 */
function elideEntryContent<T extends { entries: Array<{ id: string; type: string; raw?: unknown }> }>(
  result: T,

  return {
    ...result,
    entries: result.entries.map((entry) => elideInlineImageContentData(entry, workerId)),
  };
}

  const match = /^(?<runId>.+)-worker-\d+$/.exec(workerId);
  return match?.groups?.runId ?? null;
}

  const run = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, worker.runId)).get();
  return run ? run.id : null;
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:39:16.162Z

Since the Terminal only loads the tail of history, the config_option entry marking the session's model might fall outside that window for long conversations, leaving messages without a preceding model reference. I need a fallback to the worker's current model/effort when that happens.

I'm thinking through computing per-message attribution by scanning entries in stream order and tracking the latest config_option per worker, then falling back to the worker's launch model/effort for entries before any config_option appears in the loaded window.

But I'm not sure that fallback data is even available on the client. Maybe the safer, more honest approach is to render nothing when attribution can't be determined, rather than risk showing the wrong model — though I should first check what AgentSnapshot and ConversationMain actually pass down to Terminal about workers.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:39:39.402Z

I'm realizing AgentSnapshot's effectiveModel/effectiveEffort only reflects the live runtime state while a worker is alive, so it's best used as a fallback. I'm working out the priority order for attributing model/effort in Terminal, starting with the most recent config_option entry at or before a given message per worker.

But there's a real gap: messages before the loaded window's earliest config_option have no reliable historical evidence, and falling back to the live agent snapshot would misattribute them to the wrong model. Given the codebase's strong stance against claiming a model that never ran, I think the right approach is to attribute only when config_option evidence exists and show nothing otherwise — though that risks silently omitting labels for long conversations where the config_option falls outside the loaded tail page, so I'm considering having the server compute a full model/effort timeline from the entire JSONL instead.

One cleaner option: the entries endpoint could return a "baseline" alongside the entries — the config state in effect at the start of the returned window, derived from the last config_option at or before the first returned seq — so the client can seed its scan correctly instead of relying on stale worker-level fields like effectiveLaunchModel. I want to check how much work this would be, so I'm going to look at WorkerEntriesManager and the entry-reading code in output-store to understand the response shape.

    try {
      // Binary output is a cold, user-triggered path. Keep its DB-backed
      // resolver out of the transcript module graph so normal cursor reads
      // preserve the worker-stream hot-path invariant.
      const { readWorkerEntryContent } = await import("@/server/workers/entry-content");
      const content = await readWorkerEntryContent(workerId, contentEntryId);
      probe.mark("readContent");
      return new Response(Uint8Array.from(content.body), {
        headers: {
          "Content-Type": content.mimeType,
          "Cache-Control": "private, max-age=3600",

        },

    } catch (error) {
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "error.surfaced",
        code: "worker.output_content_unavailable",
        message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
        surface: "banner",
        runId,
        workerId,
        cause: error instanceof Error ? { name: error.name, message: error.message } : null,
      });
      return errorResponse(error, {

          ? (error as { status: number }).status
          : 500,
        source: "Worker entries",

    } finally {
      probe.end();
    }
  }
  if (url.searchParams.get("view") === "plan") {
    try {
      probe.mark("readPlan");

        kind: "error.surfaced",
        code: "worker.plan.snapshot_failed",
        message: `Failed to read the worker ACP plan: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`,
        surface: "log",
        runId,
        workerId,
        cause: error instanceof Error ? { name: error.name, message: error.message } : null,
      });
      return Response.json({ error: "Worker plan unavailable" }, { status: 503 });
    } finally {
      probe.end();
    }
  }

  const afterSeq = parseAfterSeq(url.searchParams.get("afterSeq"));
  const beforeSeq = parsePositiveInt(url.searchParams.get("beforeSeq"));
  const limitRaw = parsePositiveInt(url.searchParams.get("limit"));
  const limit = limitRaw == null ? null : Math.min(limitRaw, MAX_TAIL_LIMIT);

  try {
    // Scroll-back path: caller wants entries strictly older than beforeSeq.
    if (beforeSeq != null) {
      const result = await readWorkerEntriesBefore(runId, workerId, beforeSeq, limit ?? DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT);
      probe.mark("readBefore");
      return Response.json(elideEntryContent(result, workerId));
    }

    // Tail-first initial load: only `limit` (no afterSeq). Returns the
    // last N entries plus `hasOlder` so the client knows whether to wire
    // up scroll-back.
    if (limit != null && afterSeq === 0) {
      const tail = await readWorkerEntriesTail(runId, workerId, limit);
      if (tail) {
        probe.mark("readTail");
        return Response.json(elideEntryContent(tail, workerId));
      }
      // tail-scan couldn't prove the boundary; fall through to full read.
    }

    // Existing live-tail path: entries strictly newer than afterSeq.
    const result = await readWorkerEntriesSince(runId, workerId, afterSeq);
    probe.mark(`readEntries[${result._path ?? "?"}]`);
    return Response.json(elideEntryContent(
      { entries: result.entries, latestSeq: result.latestSeq },
      workerId,
    ));
  } catch (error) {
    return errorResponse(error, {
      status: 500,
      source: "Worker entries",
      action: "Load worker stream",
    });
  } finally {
    probe.end();
  }
};

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:39:53.520Z

Adding a server-side baseline across all three read paths feels like too much scope creep touching the hot path's caching. I'm reconsidering simpler alternatives, like tagging model/effort onto message entries at write time, but that only helps new messages and changes the stream schema. I need to check how many entries a typical session has before seq 100 and what the tail load limit actually is.

Config_option entries get re-emitted at every session start, and since worker recreation reuses the same worker id and JSONL file, mid-session model/effort changes append a new config_option in the same stream — so the timeline is accurate, but the initial config_option can page out of a long tail load. A reasonable compromise is scanning loaded entries for config_option and falling back to launch selection for anything before it, though that's not fully reliable. I'm now wondering if usage entries might carry per-turn model attribution instead, which would be a much cleaner source — let me check a real usage entry in the JSONL to see.

The exposure is pretty small. I'll add a narrow guard in `updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput` instead: if a run is `quota_waiting` and the resolved status would be `done`, don't overwrite it, since a quota-blocked worker isn't actually done. That's a cheap, clearly correct fix without over-engineering. So my focus is: implement the sweep to re-schedule due wakes for stranded implementation-runs, and guard `updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput` to prevent marking a quota-parked run as done.  * run has cycled through multiple workers (cancel → respawn). The
 * per-worker `/api/workers/:workerId/entries` endpoint only sees the
 * one worker the FE happens to be polling; after a cancel+respawn, all
 * prior worker entries disappear from view.
 *
 * Token-based pagination because seq is per-worker, not global: the
 * token encodes a per-worker seq cursor.
 */

type AfterToken = ConversationTranscriptToken;

const DEFAULT_TRANSCRIPT_LIMIT = 100;
const MAX_TRANSCRIPT_LIMIT = 1000;

function parseLimit(value: string | null): number | null {
  if (value == null) return null;
  const parsed = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
  if (!Number.isFinite(parsed) || parsed <= 0) return null;
  return Math.min(parsed, MAX_TRANSCRIPT_LIMIT);
}

    return seq > latest ? seq : latest;

}

function earliestReturnedSeq(entries: WorkerEntry[]) {

    if (seq <= 0) return earliest;
    return earliest === 0 || seq < earliest ? seq : earliest;

  if (request.method !== "GET") {
    return Response.json({ error: { code: "method_not_allowed", message: "Method not allowed." } }, {
      status: 405,
      headers: { allow: "GET" },
    });

    action: "Load conversation transcript",
  });
  probe.mark("auth");
  if (auth.response) {
    probe.end();
    return auth.response;
  }

    if (!run) {
      probe.end();
      return Response.json({ error: "Run not found" }, { status: 404 });

```text
import { eq } from "drizzle-orm";
import { parseSupersededSeqRanges, withoutSupersededEntries } from "@/lib/superseded-entries";
import { db } from "@/server/db";
import { workers } from "@/server/db/schema";
import type { WorkerEntry } from "@/server/workers/entries-types";
import { readWorkerOutputEntries } from "@/server/workers/output-store";
import { coalesceWorkerEntriesById } from "@/shared/worker-entries";

export interface ConversationTranscriptEntry extends WorkerEntry {
  workerId: string;
}

function entryTimestampMs(entry: WorkerEntry): number {
  if (!entry.timestamp) return 0;
  const ms = Date.parse(entry.timestamp);
  return Number.isFinite(ms) ? ms : 0;
}

export function compareConversationTranscriptEntries(
  left: ConversationTranscriptEntry,
  right: ConversationTranscriptEntry,
  workerCreationOrder: Map<string, number>,
): number {
  const leftTime = entryTimestampMs(left);
  const rightTime = entryTimestampMs(right);
  if (leftTime !== rightTime) return leftTime - rightTime;
  const leftOrder = workerCreationOrder.get(left.workerId) ?? Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER;
  const rightOrder = workerCreationOrder.get(right.workerId) ?? Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER;
  if (leftOrder !== rightOrder) return leftOrder - rightOrder;
  return (left.seq ?? 0) - (right.seq ?? 0);
}

export function excludeDiagnosticTranscriptEntries(entries: WorkerEntry[]) {
  return entries.filter((entry) => !entry.diagnosticOnly);
}

export function sortConversationTranscriptEntries(
  entries: ConversationTranscriptEntry[],
  workerCreationOrder: Map<string, number>,
) {
  entries.sort((left, right) => compareConversationTranscriptEntries(left, right, workerCreationOrder));
  return entries;
}

export async function readVisibleConversationTranscript(runId: string) {
  const runWorkers = await db
    .select({ id: workers.id, createdAt: workers.createdAt, supersededSeqRanges: workers.supersededSeqRanges })
    .from(workers)
    .where(eq(workers.runId, runId));
  const sortedWorkers = [...runWorkers].sort((left, right) => {
    const timeDelta = left.createdAt.getTime() - right.createdAt.getTime();
    return timeDelta !== 0 ? timeDelta : left.id.localeCompare(right.id);
  });
  const workerCreationOrder = new Map(sortedWorkers.map((worker, index) => [worker.id, index]));
  const perWorkerEntries = await Promise.all(sortedWorkers.map(async (worker) => ({
    worker,
    entries: await readWorkerOutputEntries(runId, worker.id),
  })));
  const entries: ConversationTranscriptEntry[] = [];

  for (const { worker, entries: workerEntries } of perWorkerEntries) {
    const visibleEntries = excludeDiagnosticTranscriptEntries(withoutSupersededEntries(
      workerEntries as WorkerEntry[],
      parseSupersededSeqRanges(worker.supersededSeqRanges),
    ));
    entries.push(...visibleEntries.map((entry) => ({ ...entry, workerId: worker.id })));
  }

  const workerIds = sortedWorkers.map((worker) => worker.id);
  const sortedEntries = sortConversationTranscriptEntries(entries, workerCreationOrder);
  const coalescedEntries = coalesceWorkerEntriesById(sortedEntries, workerIds) as ConversationTranscriptEntry[];
  return {
    entries: coalescedEntries,
    workerIds,
    workerCreationOrder,
  };
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:40:35.541Z

I'll handle this client-side by scanning the ordered visible entries and building a map that tracks, per worker, the latest config_option's model and effort value preceding each message—falling back to the live agent snapshot when no prior config_option exists in the loaded window.

Actually I need to reconsider the fallback: since any config change forces a worker recreation (which emits a fresh config_option), the current live effectiveModel/effectiveEffort should remain valid for all messages since the last recreation, making the fallback sound as long as I check that no config_option for that worker appears anywhere in the window.

One caveat is that the agent snapshot only exists while the worker is alive, so a finished conversation may have no primaryConversationAgent to fall back on -- in that case I just skip the label.

But there's another issue: if the primary worker changed after a cancel+respawn, entries from the older worker would wrongly inherit the new agent's config values, so I should gate the fallback on matching workerId. I also need to check that effectiveEffort is actually populated in the agent snapshot for claude sessions -- it is, since the manager sets it from session config alongside effectiveModel, both sharing the same currentValue shape as config_option.

For formatting the label like "Opus 5 (High)", I need to map the model id to a display name, but the terminal client doesn't have the full model catalog available -- I'm considering whether the config_option entry itself already carries a usable name for the current value that I could reuse instead of building a separate prettifier.

For "claude-opus-5" the choice name isn't pretty, but for "sonnet" it already reads as "Sonnet", so I need a fallback order: use a known catalog label first, then the choice name if it differs from the raw value, then a prettified id as last resort. I recall there's a `modelLabelOverrides` helper in worker-models.ts around line 139 that might already do this deriving logic, so I'm going to check if that function could be shared rather than duplicated, while being careful not to violate the guidance against parallel layers since it currently lives in src/server/.

103	function dropClaudePrefix(label: string) {

111	// "Haiku 4.5 20251001" — the date is a separate fact, not a version component.
112	function isVersionSegment(part: string) {
113	  return /^\d{1,3}$/.test(part);

131	    const separator = isVersionSegment(part) && isVersionSegment(parts[index - 1]!) ? "." : " ";

140	    "gpt-5.6-sol": "GPT-5.6 Sol",
141	    "gpt-5.6-terra": "GPT-5.6 Terra",
142	    "gpt-5.6-luna": "GPT-5.6 Luna",
143	    "gpt-6-astra": "GPT-6 Astra",
144	  };
145	  if (modelLabelOverrides[bareId]) {
146	    return modelLabelOverrides[bareId];
147	  }
148	
149	  if (!label?.trim()) {
150	    return labelFromModelId(id);
151	  }
152	
153	  return dropClaudePrefix(label.trim())
154	    .replace(/^gpt\b/i, "GPT")
155	    .replace(/\bcodex\b/i, "Codex")
156	    .replace(/\bcli\b/i, "CLI");
157	}
158	
159	function mergeModelOptions(base: WorkerModelOption[], discovered: WorkerModelOption[]) {
160	  const seen = new Set<string>();
161	  const merged: WorkerModelOption[] = [];
162	
163	  for (const model of [...base, ...discovered]) {
164	    const value = model.value.trim();
165	    if (!value || seen.has(value)) {
166	      continue;
167	    }
168	
169	    seen.add(value);
170	    merged.push({
171	      value,
172	      label: normalizeLabel(value, model.label),
173	    });
174	  }
175

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:41:02.587Z

Testing "claude-opus-5" through the label function gives "Opus 5", and for the "[1m]" context variant it produces "Opus 5[1m]" which is a bit awkward — I might want to format that as "Opus 5 (1M)" instead for readability.

Actually keeping it simple is better: I'll just strip the bracket suffix for the display label since the model identity is "Opus 5" regardless, and context window size isn't what's being asked for here. Since `worker-models.ts` on the server can't be imported into a client component, I'll extract the pure label-deriving helpers into a shared module that both sides can import from, avoiding duplicated logic.

I should double check whether normalizeLabel is exported or used elsewhere before moving it, and also think about the i18n rule — model ids themselves aren't translation keys, but the composed "{model} ({effort})" format and the effort labels like "High" are UI copy that should live in the locale files rather than being hardcoded English strings.

I'll go with `conversation.message.modelEffort` = "{model} ({effort})" and `conversation.message.model` = "{model}" for the base cases. Since the effort word itself comes from an existing helper already used untranslated elsewhere, I'm debating whether to scope-creep into adding translated effort labels too, but I'll add keys like `conversation.message.effort.low` to stay consistent with the strict i18n rule rather than reuse the existing English literal.

I'll reuse `resolveComposerEffortLabel` and just add the format key, but I need to check whether importing from `src/interface/home/utils.ts` into `Terminal.tsx` is even acceptable given the codebase's import conventions between components and interface directories. I also need to figure out where the label should actually render—the model/effort data needs to be threaded from the activity memo down into the memoized row renderer, so I'm looking at that component's structure now.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:42:06.606Z

I need to split the "message" case since user_message lacks an attribution field, so I'm building separate key strings for each case that include the relevant fields like model and effort.

Now I'm checking whether work_summary nests message items or only tools/thinking, and planning to move model-label helper functions into a shared module so worker-models.ts can reuse them. I also need to be careful that stripping the `[1m]` suffix from normalizeLabel doesn't break gateway model catalog labels.

I'll add optional raw `model` and `effort` fields to the message activity variant instead of pre-formatted labels, so rendering can format them later with the translation function. Then in Terminal.tsx I'll build an attribution map keyed by entry id while scanning visible entries for config options per worker, with a fallback to the agent snapshot, and stamp that onto message items; in ActivityRow I'll render the label beside the copy icon and reposition the container on mobile. Finally, I'll add a locale string like "{model} ({effort})" for displaying the combined attribution.

I'm now worried that switching the overlay to the left on mobile means it'll sit on top of the message's actual text rather than trailing whitespace, which is worse than the current right-side placement. Since the user did ask for this explicitly, I'm considering whether to keep it as an absolute, no-layout-cost overlay but shift it below the message on mobile instead of overlapping the last line directly.

Actually, I'll rely on the pill's semi-transparent blurred background to keep it legible over the text — that's precisely why that styling exists — and just restructure the button/label into a horizontal row with the copy notice still positioned relative to it.

Writing the positioning classes...

Now deciding on the mobile breakpoint — checking what convention the app already uses elsewhere (composer detection uses `sm:` at 640px, while WorkerOutputCopyAction uses `md:`), and I'll go with `sm:` to match the mobile viewport threshold of under 640px.

I'm keeping the icon-then-label order consistent on both mobile and desktop, left-aligned on mobile and right-aligned on desktop, since the pill sits at the right edge and reading "🗐 Opus 5 (High)" still works fine there. The "copied" notice uses absolute positioning below the span, defaulting to the left edge, which works fine given the span's new width includes the label.

Now I need to figure out how to extract the model and effort selections from the config options to compute attribution — I'm writing a helper that scans an entry's raw configOptions array, looking for options tagged with model or effort-related IDs, and pulling out the selected values for each.

Then I'm building a function that maps each message entry to its model/effort attribution by scanning entries in order and tracking the current worker's config state as it changes.

Actually, I realize `sawConfigForWorker` is redundant — since `currentByWorker.get()` already reflects whether a config was seen for that worker, I can just fall back directly when nothing's set, using the agent's snapshot values if the worker matches the primary agent.

I'm also noting that config options for a worker's later recreation appear after earlier messages, so those earlier messages need attribution based on what was known at the time, not the final config state.

Actually there's a bug: using the live snapshot as fallback means messages before an in-window config change would wrongly get the latest values instead of what was true when they ran. I need to precompute which workers have a config_option anywhere in the window, and only apply fallback for workers with none — otherwise messages before the first config_option should get null attribution.

For non-gateway Claude, the model fallback should use the agent's effective model similarly, and effort labels map through a resolver that handles low/medium/high/xhigh/max, returning null for "default" so only the model shows. Before importing utils into Terminal.tsx, I want to check whether any other src/components file already imports from @/interface, since that utils file is large.

import { type AppErrorDescriptor, normalizeAppError } from "@/lib/app-errors";
import type { ChatAttachment } from "@/lib/chat-attachments";
import { formatHumanDuration, type ConversationWorkerRecord } from "@/lib/conversation-workers";
import { isTerminalRunStatus } from "@/lib/run-status";
import { getLatestUnresolvedWorkerStuckEvent } from "@/lib/worker-stuck-events";
import { normalizeReasoningEffort } from "@/shared/reasoning-effort";
import { DEFAULT_COMPOSER_EFFORT, EFFORT_OPTIONS, RUN_PATH_PATTERN, WORKER_OPTIONS, FALLBACK_WORKER_MODEL_OPTIONS } from "./constants";
import type { AgentSnapshot, ConversationModeOption, EventStreamState, ExecutionEventRecord, MessageRecord, PlanItemRecord, PlanRecord, QueuedConversationMessageRecord, RunRecord, SupervisorInterventionRecord, WorkerModelCatalog, WorkerType } from "./types";
import { t } from "@/lib/i18n";

export { getLatestUnresolvedWorkerStuckEvent };

export type BrowserConversationRoute = {
  selectedRunId: string | null;
  draftProjectPath: string | null;
  pairTokenFromUrl: string | null;
};

export function parseBrowserConversationRoute(location: Pick<Location, "pathname" | "search">): BrowserConversationRoute {
  const pathnameMatch = location.pathname.match(RUN_PATH_PATTERN);
  const routeRunId = pathnameMatch?.[1]?.trim() || "";
  const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
  const queryRunId = params.get("run")?.trim() || "";
  const selectedRunId = routeRunId || queryRunId || null;

  return {
    selectedRunId,
    draftProjectPath: selectedRunId ? null : params.get("project")?.trim() || null,
    pairTokenFromUrl: params.get("pair")?.trim() || null,
  };
}

export function buildInlineError(
  error: unknown,
  fallback: Partial<AppErrorDescriptor> = {},
): AppErrorDescriptor {
  return normalizeAppError(error, fallback);
}

export function shouldClearMissingSelectedRunFromAuthoritativeSnapshot(args: {
=== normalizeReasoningEffort ===
/**
 * Normalize user-facing and legacy reasoning-effort aliases before they cross
 * a provider protocol boundary. UI labels intentionally remain human-readable,
 * while Codex and ACP use the compact `xhigh` enum value.
 */
export function normalizeReasoningEffort(value: string | null | undefined): string | null {
  const normalized = value?.trim().toLowerCase() || "";
  if (!normalized) {
    return null;
  }

  if (normalized === "extra high" || normalized === "extra-high") {
    return "xhigh";
  }

  return normalized;
}

  "content": "/**\n * Human-readable labels for provider model ids.\n *\n * Two surfaces name the same model and must not drift: the launch pickers,\n * which label the catalog a CLI reported, and the conversation transcript,\n * which labels the model a message was produced under. The picker path keeps\n * whatever label the provider supplied; the transcript path only ever has the\n * raw id from the session's own config, so both go through the same rules.\n */\n\n// \"Claude Opus 5\" reads as \"Opus 5\" in the picker: the vendor prefix is noise\n// next to the worker name. Only dropped when a word follows, so an id like\n// \"claude-3\" still labels as \"Claude 3\" rather than a bare \"3\".\nfunction dropClaudePrefix(label: string) {\n  return label.replace(/^claude\\s+(?=[A-Za-z])/i, \"\");\n}\n\n// Model ids spell versions with the same hyphen that separates every other\n// segment, so a plain split renders \"claude-opus-4-8\" as \"Opus 4 8\". Adjacent\n// numeric segments are one version number: rejoin them with a dot. Bounded to\n// three digits so a dated snapshot like \"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001\" reads as\n// \"Haiku 4.5 20251001\" — the date is a separate fact, not a version component.\nfunction isVersionSegment(part: string) {\n  return /^\\d{1,3}$/.test(part);\n}\n\nfunction titleCaseModelIdPart(part: string) {\n  const lower = part.toLowerCase();\n  if (lower === \"gpt\") return \"GPT\";\n  if (lower === \"cli\") return \"CLI\";\n  if (/^\\d+(?:\\.\\d+)*$/.test(part)) return part;\n  return part.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + part.slice(1);\n}\n\nfunction labelFromModelId(id: string) {\n  const bareId = id.includes(\"/\") ? id.split(\"/\").at(-1) ?? id : id;\n  const parts = bareId.split(\"-\");\n  const label = parts.reduce((accumulated, part, index) => {\n    if (index === 0) {\n      return titleCaseModelIdPart(part);\n    }\n    const separator = isVersionSegment(part) && isVersionSegment(parts[index - 1]!) ? \".\" : \" \";\n    return `${accumulated}${separator}${titleCaseModelIdPart(part)}`;\n  }, \"\");\n  return dropClaudePrefix(label);\n}\n\nexport function normalizeWorkerModelLabel(id: string, label?: string) {\n  const bareId = id.includes(\"/\") ? id.split(\"/\").at(-1) ?? id : id;\n  const modelLabelOverrides: Record<string, string> = {\n    \"gpt-5.6-sol\": \"GPT-5.6 Sol\",\n    \"gpt-5.6-terra\": \"GPT-5.6 Terra\",\n    \"gpt-5.6-luna\": \"GPT-5.6 Luna\",\n    \"gpt-6-astra\": \"GPT-6 Astra\",\n  };\n  if (modelLabelOverrides[bareId]) {\n    return modelLabelOverrides[bareId];\n  }\n\n  if (!label?.trim()) {\n    return labelFromModelId(id);\n  }\n\n  return dropClaudePrefix(label.trim())\n    .replace(/^gpt\\b/i, \"GPT\")\n    .replace(/\\bcodex\\b/i, \"Codex\")\n    .replace(/\\bcli\\b/i, \"CLI\");\n}\n\n/**\n * Label a model id read back from a live session's own config.\n *\n * The context-window marker (`claude-opus-5[1m]`) is a launch variant of one\n * model, not a different one, so it is dropped here rather than rendered as\n * \"Opus 5[1m]\". The picker keeps it: two catalog entries that differ only by\n * window would otherwise collapse into the same visible name.\n */\nexport function formatSessionModelLabel(value: string | null | undefined) {\n  const trimmed = value?.trim() || \"\";\n  if (!trimmed) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  const withoutContextMarker = trimmed.replace(/\\[[^\\]]*\\]\\s*$/, \"\").trim();\n  return normalizeWorkerModelLabel(withoutContextMarker || trimmed) || null;\n}\n"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:43:39.764Z

I'll create a new file for model attribution logic, keeping it separate and testable from Terminal.tsx, pulling in the session label formatter and effort resolver utilities.

Now I'm defining the core function that builds a map of message-to-model attribution from worker entries, with a pre-pass to track which workers have config options set, then a main pass that merges config_option updates (falling back to previous model if omitted) and assigns attribution to message entries based on the current worker state.

I need to work out how worker keys are matched when entries don't have an explicit workerId — deciding to key by `entry.workerId ?? fallback?.workerId ?? ""` so single-worker streams correctly map to the fallback worker's attribution, with a helper to check whether the fallback applies to a given key.

Writing formatting helper...

Now I want to verify how the `t()` translation function accepts parameters, so I'm checking the i18n module.

    const fallback = this.fallbackToFallbackLocale

    const template = localized ?? fallback ?? defaultMessage;

        this.missingKeys.add(key);
        this.notify();
      }
      return key;
    }
    return formatTemplate(template, params);

  "content": "/**\n * Per-message model/effort attribution for the conversation transcript.\n *\n * The only witness to what a session was actually running is the session's own\n * config, which the runtime writes into the worker stream as a `config_option`\n * entry — once at session start, and again after every\n * `session/set_config_option`. Because a model or effort change recreates the\n * worker, every change lands as a fresh `config_option` row in the same stream,\n * so scanning the stream in order attributes each message to the state that was\n * in effect when it was written.\n *\n * Nothing here guesses. A message with no evidence in the loaded window gets no\n * attribution rather than an attribution borrowed from a neighbour — labelling a\n * message with a model that never produced it is worse than labelling nothing.\n */\nimport { t } from \"@/lib/i18n\";\nimport { resolveComposerEffortLabel } from \"@/interface/home/utils\";\nimport { formatSessionModelLabel } from \"@/shared/model-label\";\nimport type { WorkerEntry } from \"@/shared/worker-entries\";\n\nexport type MessageModelAttribution = {\n  model: string | null;\n  effort: string | null;\n};\n\n/** The live worker's current selection, used only where the window has no `config_option` at all. */\nexport type MessageModelAttributionFallback = {\n  workerId: string | null;\n  model: string | null;\n  effort: string | null;\n};\n\ntype AttributionEntry = Pick<WorkerEntry, \"id\" | \"type\" | \"raw\"> & { workerId?: string | null };\n\nfunction asRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {\n  return typeof value === \"object\" && value !== null ? value as Record<string, unknown> : null;\n}\n\nfunction asNonEmptyString(value: unknown): string | null {\n  return typeof value === \"string\" && value.trim().length > 0 ? value.trim() : null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Read the model and effort selections out of a `config_option` payload.\n *\n * Config ids come from the CLI, not from us: Claude reports `effort`, older\n * Codex adapters report `reasoning_effort`. Anything else in the payload (mode,\n * permissions) is not attribution and is ignored.\n */\nexport function readConfigOptionSelections(raw: unknown): MessageModelAttribution | null {\n  const options = asRecord(raw)?.configOptions;\n  if (!Array.isArray(options)) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  let model: string | null = null;\n  let effort: string | null = null;\n  for (const option of options) {\n    const record = asRecord(option);\n    const id = asNonEmptyString(record?.id)?.toLowerCase();\n    const value = asNonEmptyString(record?.currentValue);\n    if (!id || !value) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (id === \"model\") {\n      model = value;\n    } else if (id === \"effort\" || id === \"reasoning_effort\") {\n      effort = value;\n    }\n  }\n\n  return model || effort ? { model, effort } : null;\n}\n\nexport function buildMessageModelAttribution(\n  entries: ReadonlyArray<AttributionEntry>,\n  fallback?: MessageModelAttributionFallback | null,\n): Map<string, MessageModelAttribution> {\n  const fallbackWorkerKey = fallback?.workerId ?? \"\";\n  const workerKeyOf = (entry: AttributionEntry) => entry.workerId ?? fallbackWorkerKey;\n\n  // Workers whose config is observable somewhere in this window. The fallback\n  // is only sound where it is not: a window that contains a `config_option` has\n  // a change in it, and messages written before that change ran under settings\n  // that scrolled out of view.\n  const workersWithObservedConfig = new Set<string>();\n  for (const entry of entries) {\n    if (entry.type === \"config_option\" && readConfigOptionSelections(entry.raw)) {\n      workersWithObservedConfig.add(workerKeyOf(entry));\n    }\n  }\n\n  const fallbackAttribution: MessageModelAttribution | null = fallback && (fallback.model || fallback.effort)\n    ? { model: fallback.model ?? null, effort: fallback.effort ?? null }\n    : null;\n\n  const currentByWorker = new Map<string, MessageModelAttribution>();\n  const attributionByEntryId = new Map<string, MessageModelAttribution>();\n  for (const entry of entries) {\n    const workerKey = workerKeyOf(entry);\n    if (entry.type === \"config_option\") {\n      const selections = readConfigOptionSelections(entry.raw);\n      if (!selections) {\n        continue;\n      }\n      // Merge rather than replace: a `set_config_option` response is only\n      // guaranteed to describe the option that changed.\n      const previous = currentByWorker.get(workerKey);\n      currentByWorker.set(workerKey, {\n        model: selections.model ?? previous?.model ?? null,\n        effort: selections.effort ?? previous?.effort ?? null,\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    if (entry.type !== \"message\") {\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    const observed = currentByWorker.get(workerKey);\n    const attribution = observed\n      ?? (workersWithObservedConfig.has(workerKey) || workerKey !== fallbackWorkerKey ? null : fallbackAttribution);\n    if (attribution) {\n      attributionByEntryId.set(entry.id, attribution);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return attributionByEntryId;\n}\n\n/** \"Opus 5 (High)\" — the model that produced the message, never the harness that ran it. */\nexport function formatMessageModelAttribution(\n  model: string | null | undefined,\n  effort: string | null | undefined,\n) {\n  const modelLabel = formatSessionModelLabel(model);\n  if (!modelLabel) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  const effortLabel = resolveComposerEffortLabel(effort);\n  return effortLabel\n    ? t(\"conversation.message.modelEffort\", { model: modelLabel, effort: effortLabel })\n    : modelLabel;\n}\n"
}

Now I'm wiring this into the actual `buildMessageModelAttribution` call, selecting the right source entries based on `usingUnifiedStream` and passing through the model and effort fallbacks.

Writing the attribution call...

I also need to double check that the `agent` prop passed into this component actually exposes `effectiveModel`/`effectiveEffort` on its type signature.

interface TerminalProps {
  agent?: AgentTerminalPayload | null;
  userMessages?: TerminalUserMessage[];
  /**
   * Unified worker conversation stream entries. When provided, the
   * Terminal renders directly from these and ignores `agent` /
   * `userMessages`. Phase 4 of the unified stream rollout flips
   * ConversationMain to pass this prop. See
   * docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md.
   */
  entries?: WorkerEntry[];
  /** Worker owner for per-worker streams whose entries do not carry workerId. */
  workerId?: string;
  /**
   * True when the conversation ran across more than one worker, so `seq` (which
   * restarts per worker) cannot order it. Pass the run's worker count rather
   * than letting the Terminal guess from the entries it has loaded — the guess
   * changes as pages arrive and re-sorts the list mid-read.
   */
  multiWorkerOrdering?: boolean;
  allowUserMessageFallback?: boolean;
  /**
   * Ids of user messages this client sent itself (this session). They bypass
   * `allowUserMessageFallback`: the gate protects against *stale historical*
   * `messages` rows rendering ahead of unloaded stream content, which cannot
   * apply to a message the user just typed here. Without this, the just-sent
   * bubble vanishes every time the gate closes during stream catch-up.
   */
  ungatedUserMessageIds?: ReadonlySet<string>;
  /** Ids of user messages whose send request is still in flight. */
  sendingUserMessageIds?: ReadonlySet<string>;
  getUserMessageActions?: (message: TerminalUserMessage) => TerminalUserMessageAction[];
  editingUserMessageId?: string | null;
  editingUserMessageValue?: string;
  isEditingUserMessageSaving?: boolean;
  onEditingUserMessageValueChange?: (value: string) => void;
  onCancelEditingUserMessage?: () => void;
  onSaveEditedUserMessage?: (messageId: string) => void;
  hasMoreHistory?: boolean;
  onRequestMoreHistory?: () => void;
  variant?: "terminal" | "native";
  textSizeScope?: "terminal" | "conversation";
  conversationMessageTextSize?: boolean;
  className?: string;
  showTextSizeControl?: boolean;
  showPendingAssistantIndicator?: boolean;
  pendingAssistantStatus?: TerminalPendingAssistantStatus;
  activityFilter?: (activity: TerminalActivityItem) => boolean;
  thoughtsDefaultOpen?: boolean;
  toolGroupsDefaultOpen?: boolean;
  emptyState?: ReactNode;
  isLoading?: boolean;
  projectRoot?: string | null;
  onOpenProjectFile?: (file: ProjectFileReference) => void;
  scrollAnchorKey?: string | null;
  summarizeWorkBlocks?: boolean;

(\n                    <span className=\"whitespace-nowrap text-[11px] font-medium leading-none\">\n                      {modelAttributionLabel}\n                    </span>\n                  ) : null}\n                  {copiedIdForThisRow === activity.id ? (\n                    <span\n                      role=\"status\"\n                      aria-live=\"polite\"\n                      className=\"pointer-events-none absolute left-0 top-full z-20 mt-1 whitespace-nowrap rounded-md border border-border/70 bg-popover px-2 py-1 text-[11px] font-medium leading-none text-popover-foreground shadow-sm\"\n                    >\n                      {t(\"conversation.message.copiedNotice\")}\n                    </span>\n                  ) : null}\n                </span>\n              </div>\n            ) : null}"
}

import {
  createI18nManager,
  type I18nSnapshot,
} from "@/shared/i18n-manager";
import en from "../../shared/locales/en.json";

export const OMNI_LOCALE_STORAGE_KEY = "omni-locale";

export const localeLoaders = {
  es: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/es.json")).default,
  fr: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/fr.json")).default,
  de: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/de.json")).default,
  it: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/it.json")).default,
  pt: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/pt.json")).default,
  "zh-CN": async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/zh-CN.json")).default,
  ja: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/ja.json")).default,
  ko: async () => (await import("../../shared/locales/ko.json")).default,
} as const;

const dictionaries = {
  en,
} as const;

export type OmniLocale = keyof typeof dictionaries | keyof typeof localeLoaders;

function sameItems<T>(left: readonly T[], right: readonly T[]) {
  return left.length === right.length && left.every((item, index) => Object.is(item, right[index]));
}

function sameI18nSnapshot(left: I18nSnapshot<OmniLocale>, right: I18nSnapshot<OmniLocale>) {
  return left.locale === right.locale
    && left.fallbackLocale === right.fallbackLocale
    && sameItems(left.supportedLocales, right.supportedLocales)
    && sameItems(left.loadedLocales, right.loadedLocales)
    && sameItems(left.missingKeys, right.missingKeys);
}

const createStableI18nManager = () => {
  const manager = createI18nManager<OmniLocale>({
    dictionaries,
    localeLoaders,
    fallbackLocale: "en",
    storageKey: OMNI_LOCALE_STORAGE_KEY,
  });
  const getUncachedSnapshot = manager.getSnapshot.bind(manager);
  let cachedSnapshot = getUncachedSnapshot();

  manager.getSnapshot = () => {
    const nextSnapshot = getUncachedSnapshot();

    if (sameI18nSnapshot(cachedSnapshot, nextSnapshot)) {
      return cachedSnapshot;
    }

    cachedSnapshot = nextSnapshot;
    return cachedSnapshot;
  };

  return manager;
};

export const i18nManager = createStableI18nManager();

const notifyI18nListeners = (i18nManager as unknown as { notify: () => void }).notify.bind(i18nManager);
const enqueueMicrotask = typeof queueMicrotask === "function"
  ? queueMicrotask
  : (callback: () => void) => {
      void Promise.resolve().then(callback);
    };
let translationDepth = 0;
let hasDeferredI18nNotify = false;

(i18nManager as unknown as { notify: () => void }).notify = () => {
  if (translationDepth === 0) {
    notifyI18nListeners();
    return;
  }

  if (hasDeferredI18nNotify) {
    return;
  }

  hasDeferredI18nNotify = true;
  enqueueMicrotask(() => {
    hasDeferredI18nNotify = false;
    notifyI18nListeners();
  });
};

export const t: typeof i18nManager.t = (...args) => {
  translationDepth += 1;
  try {
    return i18nManager.t(...args);
  } finally {
    translationDepth -= 1;

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:47:25.126Z

Build passes. I'm reconsidering the WorkerOutputCopyAction copy button in ConversationMain, but that's a different surface for worker output cards with its own mobile dropdown, so I'll leave it alone since the user's request is about the main transcript's agent messages. Let me double check whether the Terminal copy button is actually visible on mobile.

It's using `opacity-0` with `group-hover`, and on touch devices hover typically applies after a tap, so making it always visible on mobile like the other component does would expand scope beyond just moving it left—I'll leave the hover behavior unchanged since that's not part of the ask. The bottom-left label will overlay the last line of the message on mobile, but that's acceptable since the same was true when the button was on the right. I also want to verify visibleEntries still handles config_option rows the same way as before, and then test buildMessageModelAttribution against a real JSONL file to confirm it produces sensible output end to end.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:47:39.938Z

The real data checks out: 39 of 57 messages get attributed, and it correctly catches a mid-stream switch from Opus 5 (High) to GPT-5.6 Luna (Extra High), which is exactly the cross-harness case I wanted to verify. The 18 unattributed messages are just the ones before the first config_option shows up at seq 4, which seems like a lot but is probably explained by lifecycle events preceding it.

That still seems off though — if the first config_option is at seq 4, unattributed messages should only be seq 1-3 at most, not 18. Then I realize the Map keys by entry id, so revisions of the same message re-appended under the same id would collapse duplicates — 57 message entries could easily be fewer unique ids, which explains the gap without any actual bug. I consider spinning up the dev server to visually verify the rendering, but since the change is just CSS and a label, and both the unit tests and render tests already assert the label text and classes, I decide that's unnecessary and move to doing a final review of the diff instead.

-// "Claude Opus 5" reads as "Opus 5" in the picker: the vendor prefix is noise

-// "claude-3" still labels as "Claude 3" rather than a bare "3".
-function dropClaudePrefix(label: string) {

-// Model ids spell versions with the same hyphen that separates every other
-// segment, so a plain split renders "claude-opus-4-8" as "Opus 4 8". Adjacent
-// numeric segments are one version number: rejoin them with a dot. Bounded to
-// three digits so a dated snapshot like "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001" reads as
-// "Haiku 4.5 20251001" — the date is a separate fact, not a version component.
-function isVersionSegment(part: string) {
-  return /^\d{1,3}$/.test(part);
-}
-
-function titleCaseModelIdPart(part: string) {
-  const lower = part.toLowerCase();
-  if (lower === "gpt") return "GPT";

-  if (/^\d+(?:\.\d+)*$/.test(part)) return part;
-  return part.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + part.slice(1);
-}
-

-    }
-    const separator = isVersionSegment(part) && isVersionSegment(parts[index - 1]!) ? "." : " ";

-  return dropClaudePrefix(label);
-}
-

-    "gpt-5.6-sol": "GPT-5.6 Sol",
-    "gpt-5.6-terra": "GPT-5.6 Terra",
-    "gpt-5.6-luna": "GPT-5.6 Luna",
-    "gpt-6-astra": "GPT-6 Astra",
-  };
-  if (modelLabelOverrides[bareId]) {
-    return modelLabelOverrides[bareId];
-  }
-
-  if (!label?.trim()) {
-    return labelFromModelId(id);
-  }
-
-  return dropClaudePrefix(label.trim())

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T11:47:45.952Z

I confirm that in the legacy path, config_option entries in `agent.outputEntries` are shared between attribution scanning and protocol row rendering, so they stay consistent. I also note the live-fallback message won't get an attributed model, which is acceptable.

On the pill's padding, when there's no label the extra right padding shifts the icon slightly off the message edge, so I should make the padding conditional on the label existing via `cn()` rather than applying it unconditionally.
