## Claude · 2026-09-12T11:48:47.739Z

Both changes are in. Summary:

## 1. Copy button on the left on mobile

`src/components/Terminal.tsx:2355` — the overlay that holds the copy control moved from `bottom-0 right-0` to `bottom-0 left-0 sm:left-auto sm:right-0`. Below 640px it sits bottom-left of the agent message; from `sm` up it stays where it was. The "Copied to clipboard" toast got `left-0` so it anchors under the pill instead of drifting.

## 2. "Opus 5 (High)" next to the icon

The label renders inside the same pill as the copy icon, so it reveals on hover/tap together with it.

Where the attribution comes from: the runtime writes a `config_option` entry into the worker stream at every session start and after every `session/set_config_option` (`manager.ts:1949` and `:2089`), carrying the model and effort the session actually reports. Since changing model or effort recreates the worker into the same stream, scanning entries in order attributes each message to the state in effect when it was written — including a mid-conversation switch.

- `src/components/terminal/model-attribution.ts` (new) — the scan and the `"{model} ({effort})"` formatter.
- `src/shared/model-label.ts` (new) — the id→label rules, lifted verbatim out of `src/server/worker-models.ts` so the picker and the transcript can't drift. The transcript variant also drops the `[1m]` context marker, so `claude-opus-5[1m]` reads as "Opus 5".
- `src/lib/agent-output.ts` — optional `model`/`effort` on the message activity.
- `conversation.message.modelEffort` added to all nine locales.

Smoke-tested against a real stream in `.omniharness/run-data`: it labels 39 messages across a session that switched harness mid-conversation, producing both `Opus 5 (High)` and `GPT-5.6 Luna (Extra High)` in the right places.

Two deliberate choices worth your call:

- **No guessing.** A message with no config evidence in the loaded page gets no label rather than a borrowed one. The one fallback is the live worker's current selection, used only when the loaded window contains no `config_option` at all — which provably means nothing changed inside it. So on a long, scrolled-back conversation some older messages will show only the copy icon.
- **The effort word ("High", "Extra High") is English**, reusing `resolveComposerEffortLabel` — the same untranslated labels the composer and mobile settings summary already show. Say the word if you want those keyed per-locale.

`pnpm build:interface:web` passes, lint is clean, and 9 new tests cover the scan, the formatter and the rendered output. `tests/ui/terminal-fit.test.ts` has one failing assertion, but it fails identically on a stashed tree — it's from the uncommitted work already in your workspace, not this change.

The requested scope is the deliverable — don't quietly narrow, widen, or transform it. Interpret ambiguity the way a careful colleague would: make routine judgment calls yourself, and check in only when different readings would lead to materially different work. If you find a real problem with the task as specified, state the concern in a sentence or two, then keep building: deliver the complete work under explicitly stated assumptions, flagging important factors for the user. Finish the whole task, not just easy parts — report completion only when fully done. If part of the scope turns out to be blocked or problematic, finish every other part in full and say explicitly what you left out and why — scaling the work down is the user's call, not yours. Stop short of actions or changes clearly beyond what the user's ask implies.\n\nIf you find an uncertainty mid-task, first do everything that doesn't depend on the answer; for what does, state your assumption or ask your question to the user at the right time. Reserve blocking questions — stopping with nothing delivered until the user answers — for cases where proceeding under any assumption would be unsafe or would make the work useless if wrong.\n\nIf you raise a concern about a request and the user repeats or reaffirms it, treat that as their decision, communicate this, and proceed with the full request. Be fair and factual in resolving disagreements about the premises, scope, or approach of the work. Refusals are only for requests that are genuinely harmful or clearly prohibited, not for ordinary work that merely touches a sensitive-sounding topic. If you decline, say so plainly in a sentence, offer the nearest thing you can do, and move on without moralizing or criticism. This applies to producing work products: it doesn't override necessary refusals or the need for confirmation on risky or destructive actions.",
````text
    "# Corrections\nAvoid unnecessary or excessive self-correction. Only correct an earlier statement in your user-facing text when the error would change the user's code, conclusions, or decisions. State corrections plainly and concisely, and continue the task; combine multiple corrections rather than enumerating them all. For slips that change nothing for the user, simply make the correction and move on - no need to note it explicitly. Don't add apologies or preambles, don't be overly self-critical, and don't ruminate or give a detailed account of the mistake or tally past errors. Sometimes, other agents will report incorrect or misleading results - don't always take them at face value immediately. If other agents correct your statements and they are right, then simply update your approach without narrating too much about the correction to the user. This instruction does not apply to thinking blocks.\n\nA follow-up question about your earlier work is not, by itself, a signal that you got something wrong — answer what was asked. A statement that was accurate needs no correction: don't re-audit how you phrased it, how you verified it, or limits you already stated. When the user does point to a real error, correct it plainly as above.",
````

```text
import React from "react";
import { renderToStaticMarkup } from "react-dom/server";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  RunnerConnectionStatusPanel,
  RunnerSwitcherButton,
} from "@/interface/runners/RunnerSwitcher";

describe("runner switcher UI", () => {
  it("renders an accessible active server control without conversation counters", () => {
    const html = renderToStaticMarkup(
      <RunnerSwitcherButton
        runnerName="Studio"
        status="online"
        expanded={false}
      />,
    );

    expect(html).toContain('id="runner-switcher"');
    expect(html).toContain('aria-label="Switch server. Studio is Online."');
    expect(html).not.toContain('data-slot="badge"');
  });

  it("shows runner controls in the remote-only Electron shell", () => {
    expect(renderToStaticMarkup(
      <RunnerSwitcherButton
        runnerName="Local"
        status="online"
        expanded={false}
        surface="electron"
      />,
    )).toContain('id="runner-switcher"');
  });

  it("renders specific recovery actions for reauth, TLS, identity, incompatibility, and deferred states", () => {
    for (const [status, expected] of [
      ["needs-reauth", "Authorize again"],
      ["tls-untrusted", "Review certificate"],
      ["identity-mismatch", "Review identity"],
      ["incompatible", "Update required"],
      ["deferred", "Server is waiting"],
    ] as const) {
      const html = renderToStaticMarkup(
        <RunnerConnectionStatusPanel
          runnerName="Studio"
          status={status}
          onAction={() => {}}
        />,
      );
      expect(html).toContain(expected);
    }
  });
});
```

```text
78	// Statuses the restart route itself answers with while the runner is still
79	// alive and has stopped nothing. Its own refusals arrive under `runner.restart.*`
80	// (see `handleRunnerRestartRequest`); these three come from the auth guard in
81	// front of it, which rejects before the control service is ever called.
82	const RESTART_REFUSAL_CODES = new Set([
83	  "runtime.http_401",
84	  "runtime.http_403",
85	  "runtime.http_405",
86	]);
87	
88	/**
89	 * Did the runner refuse to restart, as opposed to going ahead and dying?
90	 *
91	 * A restart cannot report its own success. The control service acknowledges
92	 * before it stops anything, so the process answering this request is gone
93	 * moments later, and every shape that takes on the wire — a dropped connection,
94	 * a bare 502/503 from whatever still holds the port, a redirect into the
95	 * booting server's shell — is what success looks like from the browser. Only a
96	 * reply the restart route stated itself means nothing happened, and that is the
97	 * only one worth putting in front of the user.
98	 */
99	export function isRestartRefusal(error: unknown) {
100	  const code = (error as { code?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.code;
101	  if (typeof code !== "string") {
102	    return false;
103	  }
104	  return code.startsWith("runner.restart.") || RESTART_REFUSAL_CODES.has(code);
105	}
```

Actually, the restart handler already resolves the connection per-profile using the dialog's stored profileId, so that plumbing works fine. The real issue is that the Restart menu item's onClick only wires up the active run's profileId, limiting it to the currently active one rather than any profile.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:40:57.935Z

I now see the menu clearly: clicking a server switches which one is active, and the action items (Edit, Sessions, Restart, Rename, Forget) all target only that active server, with Restart further limited to same-origin servers. The user actually wants to restart a different server than the one currently serving the UI, which means I need to work around that active-server and same-origin gating.

There are two ways to solve this: remove the same-origin restriction so restart works on any active server, or add a per-server restart action directly in the list so switching isn't required. Removing the gate seems like the more direct fix, but I want to check whether that restriction exists for a technical reason — maybe the restart route or control service only works for local runners. I should look at the restart-control service and the runner's restart route to confirm.

## Claude tool result: toolu_012qj8ByWax4FSHyQB7ssZMs · 2026-09-12T13:40:57.998Z

```text
import fs from "fs/promises";
import path from "path";
import { resolveRestartControlConfig } from "@/server/restart-control";

/**
 * A runner cannot restart itself: the process serving the request is the one
 * that has to die, so it could never send a response or bring itself back. The
 * restart-control server (a separate process, port 3099 by default) owns the
 * lifecycle for exactly that reason.
 *
 * So "Restart server" in the UI proxies to that control server. The browser
 * cannot call it directly — it is a different origin and its bearer token lives
 * in a 0600 file on disk — so the runner reads the token and forwards the call.
 */
export type RunnerRestartOutcome =
  | { ok: true; pid: number | null; mode: string | null; startedAt: number | null }
  | { ok: false; code: "control_unavailable" | "control_unauthorized" | "control_failed"; message: string };

const CONTROL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;

async function readRestartToken(repoRoot: string, configuredToken: string | null) {
  if (configuredToken?.trim()) {
    return configuredToken.trim();
  }
  try {
    const raw = await fs.readFile(path.join(repoRoot, ".omniharness", "remote-restart-token"), "utf8");
    return raw.trim() || null;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

function isConnectionRefused(error: unknown) {
  const chain: unknown[] = [error, (error as { cause?: unknown } | null)?.cause];
  return chain.some((entry) => {
    const code = (entry as { code?: unknown } | null)?.code;
    const message = entry instanceof Error ? entry.message : "";
    return code === "ECONNREFUSED" || /\bECONNREFUSED\b|\bfetch failed\b/i.test(message);
  });
}

export async function requestRunnerRestart(options: {
  repoRoot?: string;
  env?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;
} = {}): Promise<RunnerRestartOutcome> {
  const repoRoot = options.repoRoot ?? process.cwd();
  const env = options.env ?? process.env;
  const doFetch = options.fetchImpl ?? fetch;
  const config = resolveRestartControlConfig(repoRoot, env);
  const token = await readRestartToken(repoRoot, config.token ?? null);

  if (!token) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      code: "control_unavailable",
      message: "The restart control service has no token on this machine. Start it with `pnpm restart:control`.",
    };
  }

  try {
    const response = await doFetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${config.port}/restart-current`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: {
        Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`,
        Accept: "application/json",
      },
      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(CONTROL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
    });

    if (response.status === 401 || response.status === 403) {
      return {
        ok: false,
        code: "control_unauthorized",
        message: "The restart control service rejected this runner's token.",
      };
    }

    if (!response.ok) {
      const detail = await response.text().catch(() => "");
      return {
        ok: false,
        code: "control_failed",
        message: `The restart control service returned ${response.status}${detail ? `: ${detail.slice(0, 200)}` : "."}`,
      };
    }

    const payload = await response.json().catch(() => ({})) as {
      pid?: unknown;
      mode?: unknown;
      startedAt?: unknown;
    };
    return {
      ok: true,
      pid: typeof payload.pid === "number" ? payload.pid : null,
      mode: typeof payload.mode === "string" ? payload.mode : null,
      startedAt: typeof payload.startedAt === "number" ? payload.startedAt : null,
    };
  } catch (error) {
    if (isConnectionRefused(error)) {
      return {
        ok: false,
        code: "control_unavailable",
        message: `No restart control service is listening on port ${config.port}. Start it with \`pnpm restart:control\`.`,
      };
    }
    return {
      ok: false,
      code: "control_failed",
      message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
    };
  }
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:41:03.477Z

I realize `requestRunnerRestart` executes on the runner itself, calling its own local restart service, which means the mechanism already supports remote operation — the browser just posts to that runner's baseUrl and the runner forwards to its own control service locally. So the `isSameOrigin` check in the UI is just a UI-level restriction rather than a real protocol constraint.

Removing that gate would let a remote server be restarted, which is what's wanted. I should check `handleRunnerRestartRequest` to see if there's a server-side same-origin or localhost restriction too.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:41:09.094Z

I see now that `enforceSameOrigin: true` is a deliberate guard in the route's auth logic — the comment explains that restarting kills every in-flight worker turn on that machine, so it's intentionally not something a bearer token from elsewhere should be able to trigger. This means the UI gate is mirroring an intentional server-side security policy, so removing it is a security-relevant decision I should surface to the user rather than quietly change.

I want to actually read the guard code to see what `enforceSameOrigin` and `requireApiSession` enforce, so I can figure out what a safe relaxation would look like.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:41:18.766Z

I see that the enforceSameOrigin check only guards against cookie-based cross-site requests as a CSRF defense — but it does nothing for bearer-token requests, which bypass it entirely. That's concerning since remote runner connections in OmniHarness authenticate specifically via bearer tokens through the credential store.

So the server-side comment claiming a bearer token "from elsewhere" can't trigger the restart route is actually wrong — enforceSameOrigin is CSRF-only and doesn't block bearer auth. I want to confirm remote runner connections really do use bearer auth by checking the RunnerConnection and runtime-api web client. If so, the real gatekeeper is the UI's `activeProfile.isSameOrigin` check, meaning the correct fix is on the UI side since the server already properly authorizes bearer-holding clients.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:41:31.315Z

I'm confirming that remote profiles authenticate via bearer token while same-origin uses cookies, meaning the CSRF guard that blocks restarts is bypassed for remote requests anyway. The UI's restriction hiding "Restart server" for non-same-origin profiles is unnecessary, so the fix is to show that control for any server.

Restart plumbing already routes through the connection registry per-profile, and `requestRunnerRestart` gracefully handles the case where the remote control service isn't running via a `control_unavailable` refusal the UI already surfaces. I should also check the restart dialog copy and locale strings to make sure it names the target server explicitly, since restarting the wrong remote machine would be a real safety concern — and I need to think through whether restart should be offered per-server in a list without requiring the user to switch the active profile first.

```text
71	export function countRunnerActivity(
72	  snapshot: RunnerConnectionSnapshot["snapshot"],
73	) {
74	  const runs = Array.isArray(snapshot?.runs)
75	    ? snapshot.runs as Array<{ status?: unknown }>
76	    : [];
77	  return runs.reduce((counts, run) => {
78	    const status = typeof run.status === "string"
79	      ? run.status.trim().toLowerCase()
80	      : "";
81	    if (status === "awaiting_user" || status === "needs_input") {

83	    } else if (
84	      status === "running"

86	      || status === "implementation"
87	    ) {
88	      counts.running += 1;
89	    }
90	    return counts;
91	  }, { needsInput: 0, running: 0 });
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:41:57.623Z

I realize I can pass the runner name through a `label` field, similar to how `openRename` works, so `openRestart` becomes `openRestart(profileId, runnerName, returnFocusId)` with `label` set to the runner name—then the restart dialog description can reference `ui.label`. Now I need to work out whether the restart action should target the selected instance or something broader.

I'm weighing whether restart should switch the active server before restarting it (simplest, matches the phrasing about "restarting a different server than the one the UI is running from"), versus allowing direct restart of any server from the list without switching—the latter being nicer but requiring a bigger UI change like a per-row submenu. I lean toward the simpler switch-then-restart approach as the more faithful minimal implementation, though I want to double check this handles the case of restarting a degraded remote server correctly.

I realize the route's same-origin check already lets bearer-authenticated requests through, meaning it already permits remote restart today — the UI gate is really the only barrier, not the API policy. So removing the gate doesn't expand what's authorized, it just exposes an existing capability, but I should fix the now-misleading comment claiming otherwise since leaving it wrong would mislead future readers. I also want to check whether the same-origin profile still shows restart when the active connection is remote.

Also, in Electron every profile is remote-only since nothing's served locally, so today Electron users can't restart any server at all — removing the gate would fix that too. I want to check `RunnerRegistryProvider.tsx` around lines 100-180 to confirm this.

```text
function validateSessionTransport(
  request: Request,
  session: ActiveAuthSession,
  usedBearer: boolean,
) {
  const expectedTransport = usedBearer ? "bearer" : "cookie";
  if (session.transport !== expectedTransport) {
    return jsonError(401, "Auth", "Validate session", "Authentication required.");
  }
  if (!usedBearer) {
    return null;
  }
  if (!isSecureOrLoopbackRequest(getRequestNetworkIdentity(request))) {
    return jsonError(403, "Auth", "Validate session", "Bearer sessions require HTTPS for non-loopback clients.");
  }

  const origin = request.headers.get("origin")?.trim() ?? null;
  if (session.clientKind === "native") {
    return origin
      ? jsonError(403, "Auth", "Validate session", "Native sessions cannot be used by browser-origin requests.")
      : null;
  }
  if (!origin || origin === "null" || origin !== session.boundOrigin) {
    return jsonError(403, "Auth", "Validate session", "Bearer session origin rejected.");
  }
  return null;
}

function getSessionCookieValue(request: Request) {
  const cookieHeader = request.headers.get("cookie") ?? "";
  for (const part of cookieHeader.split(";")) {
    const separator = part.indexOf("=");
    if (separator < 0) {
      continue;
    }
    const name = part.slice(0, separator).trim();
    if (name === AUTH_SESSION_COOKIE) {
      return decodeURIComponent(part.slice(separator + 1).trim());
    }
  }
  return null;
}
```

```text
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { createFetchRuntimeRequest } from "@/runtime-api/request";
import { isRestartRefusal } from "@/interface/runners/RunnerControls";

async function rejectionFrom(fetchImpl: typeof fetch) {
  return createFetchRuntimeRequest({ fetchImpl, surface: "web" })("POST", "/api/runner/restart")
    .then(() => null, (error: unknown) => error);
}

describe("isRestartRefusal", () => {
  it("surfaces a refusal the restart route stated itself", async () => {
    const refusal = await rejectionFrom(async () => new Response(
      JSON.stringify({
        error: {
          code: "runner.restart.control_unavailable",
          message: "No restart control service is listening on port 3099.",
        },
      }),
      { status: 503 },
    ));

    expect(isRestartRefusal(refusal)).toBe(true);
  });

  it("surfaces an auth rejection that stopped the request before the control service", async () => {
    const rejected = await rejectionFrom(async () => new Response(
      JSON.stringify({ error: { message: "Authentication required." } }),
      { status: 403 },
    ));

    expect(isRestartRefusal(rejected)).toBe(true);
  });

  it("treats a dying or returning server as a restart in progress", async () => {
    // The connection cut before any reply.
    expect(isRestartRefusal(await rejectionFrom(async () => {
      throw new TypeError("Failed to fetch");
    }))).toBe(false);

    // A bare gateway status from whatever still answers on the port. The old
    // rule surfaced this as "The server operation failed" on restarts that
    // had in fact worked.
    expect(isRestartRefusal(await rejectionFrom(async () => new Response("", { status: 502 })))).toBe(false);
    expect(isRestartRefusal(await rejectionFrom(async () => new Response("", { status: 503 })))).toBe(false);

    // The booting server redirecting into its app shell.
    expect(isRestartRefusal(await rejectionFrom(async () => new Response("", {
      status: 302,
      headers: { location: "/" },
    })))).toBe(false);
  });

  it("treats missing and malformed rejections as a restart in progress", () => {
    expect(isRestartRefusal(undefined)).toBe(false);
    expect(isRestartRefusal(null)).toBe(false);
    expect(isRestartRefusal(new Error("boom"))).toBe(false);
    expect(isRestartRefusal({ code: 503 })).toBe(false);
  });
});
```

I'll write a test rendering RunnerControls inside RunnerRegistryProvider with fake registry/profileStore/credentialStore for a remote active profile, checking that the Restart item appears, plus a same-origin case to confirm no regression. I should also check whether DropdownMenuContent renders its content when closed, since Radix/base-ui dropdowns typically don't render closed content into the DOM.

I'm weighing test strategies: verifying RunnerUiManager.openRestart directly is straightforward, but asserting the menu item's JSX gate is trickier given portal-based rendering. Let me just try an SSR render first to see whether the dropdown content actually shows up.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:43:44.247Z

Since Portal content won't render in SSR when closed, I can't reliably test the menu item that way. Instead I'll write a real unit test for `openRestart` in `runner-ui-manager.test.ts`, plus a source-level guard test confirming the restart menu item is no longer gated on `isSameOrigin`, following the pattern used in `terminal-fit.test.ts`.

That source-assertion test is fragile though, and it's already broken in this workspace, so I'm hesitant. I considered extracting the restart decision into a pure exported function like `canRestartRunner(connection)`, but since the condition is now unconditional there's nothing left to test that way.

The real testable behaviors are that `openRestart` now carries the name, and that `handleDialogAction` restarts the dialog's profile rather than the active one -- though that second one is existing, untested behavior buried in an inline `useCallback` and not easily testable. I'll add the ui-manager test, update existing tests, and check whether any other caller of `openRestart` exists before running the runner tests.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T13:44:19.031Z

I'm double-checking that switching to a remote server correctly routes the restart through the runtime API tied to the profile, and confirming the restart request hits the remote runner's endpoint with proper authentication before it forwards onward.

Tracing the error path, though, I notice a problem: if the remote machine doesn't have its control service running, the refusal gets swallowed by a generic error handler instead of surfacing something like "no control service running." Since remote servers often won't have that service active, this vague error would confuse users trying to restart — I want to fix this by detecting restart-specific refusal codes and showing a clearer message instead.

Since `t()` just returns raw keys for anything missing, I'll need to add locale entries for the control_unavailable, control_unauthorized, and control_failed cases so they render properly rather than showing the raw key string.

## Claude · 2026-09-12T13:44:59.522Z

Done. You can now restart any server you're connected to, not just the one serving the page.

## What was blocking it

Only the UI. `RunnerControls.tsx:458` hid "Restart server" behind `activeProfile.isSameOrigin`. Everything underneath was already per-server: the confirm handler resolves the connection for the dialog's own `profileId`, and `POST /api/runner/restart` runs *on the target runner*, which forwards to the restart-control service on its own `127.0.0.1:3099` (`src/server/runner/restart-request.ts:63`). Nothing about the mechanism assumed local.

The route's `enforceSameOrigin: true` looked like a second blocker, but it isn't — `requireApiSession` skips that check for bearer requests (`src/server/auth/guards.ts:152`), and remote runner connections are exactly bearer. Those tokens are origin-bound, require HTTPS off-loopback, and only exist because you explicitly authorized that server. So no server change was needed; I corrected the comment there, which claimed the opposite of what the code does and would have misled the next reader.

## Changes

- **`RunnerControls.tsx`** — restart is offered for whichever server is selected. Pick the server in the switcher, then Restart.
- **`RunnerUiManager.openRestart`** now takes the runner name, and the confirmation shows a "Server to restart" block with it. Worth having: the description is generic, and with remote targets you should not be guessing which machine you're about to stop.
- **Error surfacing** — a refusal the restart route stated itself now shows its own reason instead of "The server operation failed". The common one is no control service running on that machine; three new locale keys cover `control_unavailable`, `control_unauthorized`, `control_failed`.

A side effect worth knowing: the same-origin gate also hid restart from the Electron and Capacitor shells entirely, since their local server profile is `isSameOrigin: false` (`RunnerRegistryProvider.tsx:172`). Those get the action now too.

`pnpm build:interface:web` passes; typecheck, lint, and the 62 runner/i18n tests pass, with a new case covering the dialog's target name.

Two things to flag:

- **The target machine needs `pnpm restart:control` running.** Without it you get the new "No restart control service is running on that server" message rather than a restart. Nothing here starts it remotely.
- **Restart still acts on the *selected* server**, so you switch to it first. If you'd rather restart a server straight from the list without making it active, that's a per-row submenu in the switcher — say the word and I'll add it.

uuid: bd44c73e-2c19-40ca-b8de-5f69fc198344
parent: b207e7c7-02c2-4eee-8202-57468fb8dfb5

<!-- /codex-event -->
